\begin{lemma}[Certified population full-rank branch at \(z=1/2\)]\label{lem:z-half-diagonal-branch-certificate-main}
Let
\[
z=\frac12,\qquad \theta=\log2,\qquad
g_0=\frac{1-\log2}{2},\qquad g_1=\frac14,
\]
\[
\mu=2+2\log2,\qquad m_2=g_1+g_0^2 .
\]
Evaluate the displayed finite matrices \(A_z,B_z,e_z,d_z\) and the Jacobian
\(D\Phi(r(z))\) by the directed interval arithmetic transcript in
\Cref{tab:diagonal-branch-transcript-main}, with
rounding mode \(R\) equal to downward rounding for lower endpoints and upward
rounding for upper endpoints, and precision \(P=90\) significant decimal
digits.  The supplementary material gives the machine replay of this
population branch transcript, including the exact JSON transcript and
environment.  Those replay files support the printed interval arithmetic only.
The resulting enclosure for
\[
\Sigma_\sigma(1/2)
=D\Phi(r(1/2))\Sigma_r(1/2)D\Phi(r(1/2))^\top
\]
satisfies
\[
\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_\sigma(1/2)
\in[36.95298777813731,36.95298777813732],
\]
\[
\det\Sigma_\sigma(1/2)
\in[1.372485374000321,1.372485374000322].
\]
Therefore \(z=1/2\) belongs to the certified domain \(Z_{\rm cert}\), and the
certified population branch at this candidate is the full-rank branch:
\[
\operatorname{rank}\Sigma_\sigma(1/2)=2,qquad
\lambda_{\min}\{\Sigma_\sigma(1/2)\}>0.037.
\]
This certificate supplies only the covariance input used by Theorem~E's
pointwise projection example.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The preceding transcript is an outward-rounded directed interval evaluation of
the finite D-MAP covariance and quotient-sandwich formulas, with the exact
input singleton \([z]=[0.5,0.5]\).  Directed outward rounding at every
arithmetic operation ensures that the determinant enclosure contains the true determinant
\(\det\Sigma_\sigma(1/2)\).  Its lower endpoint is larger than \(1.37\), and
therefore
\[
[I_{\det}]\subset[1.37,\infty).
\]
Thus both eigenvalues are positive.  Moreover, for a positive definite
\(2\times2\) matrix,
\[
\lambda_{\min}=\frac{\det\Sigma_\sigma}{\lambda_{\max}}
\ge\frac{\det\Sigma_\sigma}{\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_\sigma}
>\frac{1.372485374000321}{36.95298777813732}>0.037.
\]
This gives the asserted rank and quantitative nondegeneracy.  Since the transcript is attached
only to the singleton input interval, it certifies no candidate other than
\(z=1/2\).  There is therefore no terminal subinterval list for a continuum
\(Z_{\rm cert}\) in this appendix; any later interval-valued certified domain
would have to print its terminal subintervals and margins separately.
\end{proof}


 succeeded in 157ms:
\section{Killed-reset fibres: an exact two-state arc and higher-state structure}

The minimal-realization statements in this subsection are recalled background,
not contributions of this paper.  Kalman's similarity theorem is the classical
source, and minimal MAP/phase-type formulations and canonical-coordinate
developments are given in
\cite{Kalman1963LinearSystems,TelekHorvath2007MinimalMAP,
BladtNielsen2017MatrixExponential,MeszarosTelek2013DiscreteMAP2Canonical,
PappTelek2013DiscretePHCanonical}.  We state the consequence needed to locate
the visible fibre, classify the complete Markovian fibre of the physical
two-state kernel, and then prove the higher-state quantitative assertion: at a
minimal interior killed-reset kernel the admissible fibre has local dimension
\((n-1)^2\).  Nonunique MAP and Coxian representatives are documented in
\cite{RamirezCoboLilloWiper2010MAP2Nonidentifiability,
RamirezCoboLillo2012WeakEquivalentMAP,RizkBurkeWalsh2019CoxianNonuniqueness}.
Graph-specific time-to-event inverses are treated algebraically by
Radulescu et al.\ \cite{RadulescuEtAl2025TimeToEventIdentifiability}; the
criterion below instead isolates the exact orbit fibre for any declared
minimal killed-reset subclass.

\paragraph{Recalled minimal-realization uniqueness.}
Let \((a,A,b)\) and \((\widetilde a,\widetilde A,\widetilde b)\) be two real
scalar realizations of the same dimension \(n\) such that
\[
aA^k b=\widetilde a\widetilde A^k\widetilde b
\qquad(k\ge0).
\]
Assume that both reachability matrices
\[
[\,b,Ab,\ldots,A^{n-1}b\,],\qquad
[\,\widetilde b,\widetilde A\widetilde b,\ldots,
\widetilde A^{n-1}\widetilde b\,]
\]
and both observability matrices
\[
\begin{bmatrix}a\\aA\\\vdots\\aA^{n-1}\end{bmatrix},
\qquad
\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde a\\\widetilde a\widetilde A\\\vdots\\
\widetilde a\widetilde A^{n-1}\end{bmatrix}
\]
have rank \(n\). Then there is a unique \(M\in\operatorname{GL}_n(\RR)\)
such that
\[
\widetilde a=aM,
\qquad \widetilde A=M^{-1}AM,
\qquad \widetilde b=M^{-1}b.
\]
These rank hypotheses are precisely properties \textup{(R)} and \textup{(L)}
of Bladt and Nielsen, whose Theorem~4.2.16 characterizes minimality by their
simultaneous validity; the corresponding intertwining relations are given in
their Lemmas~4.2.5--4.2.6
\cite[Theorem~4.2.16, p.~216, and Lemmas~4.2.5--4.2.6,
p.~212]{BladtNielsen2017MatrixExponential}. This standard result is invoked
here and is not claimed as a new theorem; see also Kalman
\cite{Kalman1963LinearSystems}, Telek--Horv\'ath
\cite{TelekHorvath2007MinimalMAP}, and
\cite{Ryden1996Aggregated,OCinneide1989PhaseTypeNonuniqueness,
OCinneide1991InvariantPolytopes}.

\begin{proposition}[Recalled orbit-fibre consequence of minimal realization]
\label{thm:nstate-killed-reset-identifiability-dichotomy-main}
Fix \(n\ge2\), put \(\beta=e_n^\top\), and let
\[
\mathcal K_n=\{K\in\RR^{n\times n}:K\ge0,\ K\mathbf1\le\mathbf1,\
\operatorname{spr}(K)<1\}.
\]
For \(K\in\mathcal K_n\), define the killed-reset D-MAP by
\[
T_0=K,\qquad T_1=(I-K)\mathbf1\beta
\]
and its Palm survival coordinates by \(S_k(K)=\beta K^k\mathbf1\).
Call \(K\) minimal when both
\[
\mathcal C(K)=[\,\mathbf1,K\mathbf1,\ldots,K^{n-1}\mathbf1\,],
\qquad
\mathcal O(K)=
\begin{bmatrix}\beta\\ \beta K\\ \vdots\\ \beta K^{n-1}\end{bmatrix}
\]
have rank \(n\). Let
\[
\mathcal G_n=\{M\in\operatorname{GL}_n(\RR):
M\mathbf1=\mathbf1,\ \beta M=\beta\},
\]
and let \(\mathfrak P_n\subset\mathcal G_n\) be the reset-preserving
permutation matrices.

For any declared subclass \(\mathcal C\subset\mathcal K_n\) of minimal
kernels, fix the structural equivalence \(\sim_{\mathcal C}\) under which
identification is requested, and assume that \(K\sim_{\mathcal C}L\) implies
equality of visible laws. Examples are reset-preserving hidden-state
relabeling and, in a serial rate chart, permutation of the rate coordinates.
Define the Markovian orbit fibre
\[
\mathfrak F_{\mathcal C}(K)=
\left\{M^{-1}KM\in\mathcal C:M\in\mathcal G_n\right\}
\big/\!\sim_{\mathcal C}.
\]
Then standard minimal-realization uniqueness gives the following exact
characterization.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item Two minimal kernels \(K,L\in\mathcal K_n\) generate the same stationary
visible click law if and only if
\[
\begin{aligned}
L&=M^{-1}KM &&\text{for the unique }M\in\mathcal G_n\\
&&&\text{implementing their normalized minimal realizations}.
\end{aligned}
\]
\item Equivalently, the declared hidden object is identifiable from the visible law within
\(\mathcal C\), modulo \(\sim_{\mathcal C}\), if and only if
\[
\#\mathfrak F_{\mathcal C}(K)=1\qquad\text{for every }K\in\mathcal C.
\]
Pointwise identifiability at \(K\) is equivalent to the same condition at
that \(K\); if the cardinality exceeds one, its distinct classes are exactly
the hidden nonidentifiability fibre. Thus this assertion is the orbit-fibre
reformulation of part \textup{(i)}.
\end{enumerate}
Part~\textup{(ii)} is deliberately recorded only as terminology: after the
fibre has been defined, singleton fibre and identifiability modulo
\(\sim_{\mathcal C}\) are the same assertion.  Neither part is claimed as a
new realization theorem.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Deterministic reset makes the visible process renewal. Its Palm gap tail is
exactly \(S_k(K)\), so equality of visible stationary laws is equivalent to
equality of all the scalar coordinates \(\beta K^k\mathbf1\), \(k\ge0\).
Apply the minimal-realization theorem stated above to the triples
\((\beta,K,\mathbf1)\) and \((\beta,L,\mathbf1)\). Its hypotheses are exactly
the two displayed rank conditions in the proposition, and it gives a unique
similarity \(L=M^{-1}KM\). Preservation of the fixed input and output vectors
gives \(M\mathbf1=\mathbf1\) and \(\beta M=\beta\), respectively.
Conversely, these two identities give
\[
\beta(M^{-1}KM)^k\mathbf1=\beta K^k\mathbf1
\]
for every \(k\), proving (i). Taking the intersection with
\(\mathcal C\) and then quotienting precisely the declared structural
equivalence proves
the necessary-and-sufficient assertion (ii).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Complete two-state fibre arc and exchange dichotomy]
\label{thm:two-state-complete-fibre-arc-main}
Under \Cref{ass:model-inference-contract-main}, put \(K=T_0\),
\(q_\ast=\Gamma/\kappa_{\mathrm r}\), and
\[
 I_\ast=[\min\{1,q_\ast\},\max\{1,q_\ast\}].
\]
Then \(K\) is minimal, and its complete visible-law fibre in
\(\mathcal K_2\) is the injectively parametrized closed arc
\[
 \{K(q):q\in I_\ast\},
 \qquad
 K(q)=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 p-b+bq & \displaystyle\frac{b(1-q)(q-q_\ast)}q\\[5pt]
 bq & s+b(1-q)
 \end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{F1}
\]
Here complete means that every \(L\in\mathcal K_2\) with the same stationary
visible click law as \(K\) is \(K(q)\) for exactly one \(q\in I_\ast\).
Writing \(T_0(\alpha,\eta)\) for the no-click kernel constructed with attempt
rate \(\alpha\) and recovery rate \(\eta\), the endpoints are the two physical
triangular kernels obtained by exchanging those rates:
\[
 K(1)=T_0(\Gamma,\kappa_{\mathrm r}),
 \qquad
 K(q_\ast)=T_0(\kappa_{\mathrm r},\Gamma).
 \tag{F2}
\]
If \(\Gamma\ne\kappa_{\mathrm r}\), every \(q\) strictly between the
endpoints gives a strictly positive kernel with strictly positive killing
deficits, and the physical triangular subfamily meets the arc only at the two
endpoints.  If \(\Gamma=\kappa_{\mathrm r}\), then \(I_\ast=\{1\}\), so
the entire two-state killed-reset fibre is a singleton.  Thus the unrestricted
two-state hidden kernel is identifiable exactly on the exchange diagonal and
has a continuum fibre off it; within the physical sampled-counter subfamily,
the only ambiguity off the diagonal is the rate exchange.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
First \(b>0\).  If \(\Gamma\ne\kappa_{\mathrm r}\), direct evaluation of
the defining integral gives
\[
 b=\frac{\kappa_{\mathrm r}(p-s)}{\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma},
 \qquad
 b+s-p=\frac{\Gamma(p-s)}{\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma}
          =bq_\ast>0.
 \tag{F3}
\]
The same identities hold by continuity on the diagonal, where
\(p=s\), \(b=\Gamma\Delta\tau p>0\), and \(q_\ast=1\).  Consequently
\[
 \det[\mathbf1,K\mathbf1]=b+s-p=bq_\ast>0,
 \qquad
 \det\begin{bmatrix}e_2^\top\\e_2^\top K\end{bmatrix}=-b\ne0,
\]
so \(K\) is minimal.

Every member of \(\mathcal G_2\) has the unique form
\[
 M_q=\begin{pmatrix}q&1-q\\0&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad q\ne0.
\]
Multiplication, using \(b+s-p=bq_\ast\), gives
\(M_q^{-1}KM_q=K(q)\) in \textup{(F1)}.  Since \(K\) is minimal, its scalar
Hankel sequence has rank two.  Any \(L\in\mathcal K_2\) with the same visible
law has the same leading Hankel matrix; its factorization as
\(\mathcal O(L)\mathcal C(L)\) has rank two, so both factors have rank two and
\(L\) is minimal as well.  The recalled orbit--fibre proposition therefore
forces every such \(L\) to have the displayed form.  If
\(K(q)\in\mathcal K_2\), its lower-left entry
is \(bq\ge0\).  Invertibility excludes \(q=0\), hence \(q>0\).  Its
upper-right entry then yields the exact condition
\[
 \frac{b(1-q)(q-q_\ast)}q\ge0
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad q\in I_\ast.
 \tag{F4}
\]

Conversely, let \(q\in I_\ast\).  The two diagonal entries in
\textup{(F1)} are affine in \(q\), with endpoint values \(p,s\) and \(s,p\),
respectively, and are therefore positive.  The lower-left entry is positive,
and \textup{(F4)} makes the upper-right entry nonnegative.  Writing
\(a=1-b-s>0\), the two killing deficits are
\[
 \mathbf1-K(q)\mathbf1
 =\begin{pmatrix}a+bq_\ast/q\\a\end{pmatrix}>0.
 \tag{F5}
\]
Similarity preserves the spectral radius
\(\max\{p,s\}<1\), so \(K(q)\in\mathcal K_2\).  This proves that the
displayed arc is the complete fibre.  It is injective because its lower-left
entry is \(bq\).

At \(q=1\) one recovers \(K\).  At \(q=q_\ast\),
\[
 K(q_\ast)=\begin{pmatrix}s&0\\bq_\ast&p\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Equation~\textup{(F3)} says that \(bq_\ast\) is precisely the no-click
recovery-to-ready probability after exchanging the two rates, and its second
row has the same killing deficit \(a\); this proves \textup{(F2)}.  If
\(q_\ast\ne1\), the product in the upper-right entry is strictly positive
inside \(I_\ast\) and vanishes only at its endpoints.  All remaining entries
and both deficits are already strict there.  If \(q_\ast=1\),
\textup{(F4)} forces \(q=1\).  The endpoint, triangular-intersection, and
identifiability statements follow.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[All-collision serial identifiability]
\label{cor:nstate-serial-all-collision-identifiability-main}
Fix \(n\ge2\), a known \(\Delta\tau>0\), and arbitrary positive rates
\(\theta=(\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_n)\), with repetitions allowed. Let
\[
Q(\theta)_{ii}=-\theta_i,\qquad
Q(\theta)_{i,i-1}=\theta_i\quad(2\le i\le n),
\]
with all other entries zero, and put
\[
T_0(\theta)=e^{\Delta\tau Q(\theta)},\qquad
T_1(\theta)=(I-T_0(\theta))\mathbf1e_n^\top.
\]
Then the visible record identifies exactly the unordered rate multiset
\(\{\!\{\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_n\}\!\}\). Equivalently, the fibre of the
labelled serial parameter is its \(\mathfrak S_n\)-permutation orbit.

More explicitly, if the distinct rates are
\(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_r\) with multiplicities
\(m_1,\ldots,m_r\), and \(z_j=e^{-\lambda_j\Delta\tau}\), then the Palm tail
has the confluent Prony form
\[
S_k=\sum_{j=1}^r\sum_{\ell=0}^{m_j-1}
c_{j\ell}k^\ell z_j^k,\qquad c_{j,m_j-1}\ne0,
\]
and its minimal recurrence polynomial is
\[
\chi_\theta(x)=\prod_{j=1}^r(x-z_j)^{m_j}.
\]
The leading \(n\times n\) Hankel matrix
\((S_{i+j})_{0\le i,j<n}\) is invertible; hence
\(S_0,\ldots,S_{2n-1}\) determine \(\chi_\theta\), including all collision
multiplicities. Pole collision therefore changes conditioning and the local
root chart, but does not change population identifiability.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Starting in phase \(n\), the absorption time is
\(W=X_n+\cdots+X_1\), where the \(X_i\) are independent exponentials with
rates \(\theta_i\). Its Laplace transform is
\[
\EE e^{-sW}=\prod_{i=1}^n\frac{\theta_i}{s+\theta_i}.
\]
Consequently
\[
\int_0^\infty e^{-st}\PP(W>t)\,dt
=\frac{1-\prod_{i=1}^n\theta_i/(s+\theta_i)}{s}
\]
has at
\(-\lambda_j\) a pole of exact order \(m_j\). Partial-fraction inversion and
sampling at \(t=k\Delta\tau\) give the displayed exponential-polynomial form
with nonzero top confluent coefficient. Standard confluent Prony algebra
then gives the exact minimal annihilator \(\chi_\theta\)
\cite{KunisRoemerVonDerOhe2020Prony}.

For completeness of the finite-coordinate assertion, the matrices
\(\mathcal O(Q)\) and \(\mathcal C(Q)\) are nonsingular: successive rows
\(e_n^\top Q^j\) and successive columns \(Q^j\mathbf1\) expose the nonzero
bidiagonal products triangularly. Because the exponential is injective on
the real spectrum of \(Q\), Hermite interpolation supplies a polynomial
\(p\) with \(Q=p(e^{\Delta\tau Q})\); hence the corresponding observation and
reachability spaces for \(T_0=e^{\Delta\tau Q}\) are also full. Therefore
\[
(S_{i+j})_{0\le i,j<n}=\mathcal O(T_0)\mathcal C(T_0)
\]
is invertible, and the first \(2n\) tails solve uniquely for the order-\(n\)
recurrence. The roots and their algebraic multiplicities recover
\(\lambda_j=-\Delta\tau^{-1}\log z_j\) and \(m_j\). Conversely, permuting
the independent summands does not change \(W\), the Palm gap law, or the
stationary renewal law. This proves the exact fibre claim.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Quantitative interior fibre dimension and positivity persistence]
\label{thm:nstate-interior-continuum-nonidentifiability-main}
For the unrestricted class with \(\sim_{\mathcal K_n}\) equal to
reset-preserving state relabelling, the hidden kernel is locally
nonidentifiable at every minimal interior point \(K\), where interior means
\(K>0\) and \((I-K)\mathbf1>0\) entrywise. More precisely, the
reset-preserving similarity group has Lie algebra
\[
\mathfrak g_n=\{B:B\mathbf1=0,\ \beta B=0\},
\qquad \dim\mathfrak g_n=(n-1)^2,
\]
and the orbit map
\[
\psi_K:\mathcal G_n\longrightarrow\mathbb R^{n\times n},\qquad
\psi_K(M)=M^{-1}KM,
\]
has injective differential
\[
D\psi_K(I)B=[K,B]\qquad(B\in\mathfrak g_n).
\]
Consequently the visible fibre through \(K\) contains, in a neighbourhood of
\(K\), a smooth immersed manifold of dimension exactly \((n-1)^2\).  Every
kernel on a sufficiently small neighbourhood of this orbit manifold remains
strictly substochastic: its entries and killing deficits are positive.  After
quotienting by the finite reset-preserving permutation group, the local fibre
still has dimension \((n-1)^2\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Because \(\beta\mathbf1=1\),
\(\RR^n=\operatorname{span}\{\mathbf1\}\oplus\ker\beta\).  Relative to this
decomposition, \(B\mathbf1=0\) removes the first block column and
\(\beta B=0\) forces the range into \(\ker\beta\).  Thus
\(B=\operatorname{diag}(0,A)\), with arbitrary
\(A\in\operatorname{End}(\ker\beta)\), and
\(\dim\mathfrak g_n=(n-1)^2\).

Differentiating \(\exp(-tB)K\exp(tB)\) at zero gives
\(D\psi_K(I)B=[K,B]\).  Suppose \([K,B]=0\).  Since \(B\mathbf1=0\),
\[
BK^j\mathbf1=K^jB\mathbf1=0,
\qquad j=0,\ldots,n-1.
\]
The reachability matrix
\([\mathbf1,K\mathbf1,\ldots,K^{n-1}\mathbf1]\) has rank \(n\), so these
equalities imply \(B=0\).  Hence the differential is injective on all of
\(\mathfrak g_n\), not merely in one selected direction.

The constant-rank theorem now makes the image of \(\psi_K\) near the identity
a smooth immersed manifold of dimension \((n-1)^2\).  For every
\(M\in\mathcal G_n\),
\[
\beta\psi_K(M)^j\mathbf1=\beta K^j\mathbf1\qquad(j\ge0),
\]
so the recalled orbit-fibre result places this entire manifold in the visible
fibre.  Finally, all entries of \(M^{-1}KM\) and all components of
\((I-M^{-1}KM)\mathbf1\) depend continuously on \(M\).  Their values at
\(M=I\) are strictly positive; a sufficiently small identity neighbourhood
therefore remains inside the interior Markovian cone.  The stabilizer has zero
Lie algebra by injectivity of the differential, and quotienting by the finite
reset-preserving permutation group cannot change local dimension.  This proves
all assertions.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Nonminimal and graph-specific open interfaces]
\label{rem:nstate-identifiability-open-interfaces-main}
The characterization and the two headline results deliberately assume
Hankel/minimal rank \(n\). If a nominal
\(n\)-state representation has lower visible rank, equal laws need not be
related by an invertible \(n\times n\) similarity; a sharp classification then
requires a stratified treatment of nonminimal augmentations. For a fixed
nonserial zero pattern on the boundary of the Markovian cone, evaluating the
orbit intersection \(\mathfrak F_{\mathcal C}(K)\) is an exact semialgebraic
problem, but no graph-uniform closed form is claimed here. Finally, collision
multiplicity is population-identifiable in the serial model but statistically
singular under noisy finite records. These are explicit interfaces, not
consequences of the present results.
\end{remark}

For orientation, the unrestricted fibre is already nontrivial in two states:
\[
K=\begin{pmatrix}1/2&0\\1/2&1/4\end{pmatrix},\qquad
M=\begin{pmatrix}9/10&1/10\\0&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad
M^{-1}KM=\begin{pmatrix}9/20&1/45\\9/20&3/10\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Both kernels are nonnegative and substochastic,
\(M\mathbf1=\mathbf1\), \(e_2^\top M=e_2^\top\), and their entire visible
renewal laws coincide, although the hidden kernels do not.

# Deep exploration: quotient and fibre candidates

Checked 2026-08-08. Scores are on a 1--5 scale. API searches used the arXiv
Atom API, Crossref, the Semantic Scholar Graph API, and zbMATH Open. Search
results delimit the claim; they do not prove absence from the literature.

| Candidate | Reach | Novelty | Value | Assessment and nearest prior work |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Complete physical D-MAP(2) killed-reset fibre, including exchange-diagonal dichotomy | 5 | 4 | 5 | Proved below. The general phenomenon is close to Ramirez-Cobo--Lillo--Wiper (2010), *Nonidentifiability of the Two-State Markovian Arrival Process*, DOI `10.1239/jap/1285335400`, and Ramirez-Cobo--Lillo (2012), DOI `10.1007/s11009-011-9227-x`. Neither API record states this sampled-counter arc or its singleton exchange stratum. |
| Exact tangent kernel and quotient dimension \(2n-1\) at every minimal interior kernel | 4 | 2 | 3 | Likely reachable by differentiating normalized minimal realizations. The substance is classical realization-manifold geometry: Kalman uniqueness, Telek--Horvath minimal MAPs, and Bladt--Nielsen. It would mostly repackage the already exact orbit theorem. |
| A finite \(2n\)-tail certificate for equality of minimal visible laws | 5 | 1 | 3 | Immediate from Hankel recurrence/Ho--Kalman machinery. Gupta--Fairman (1973), *An explicit approach to minimal realization from system Markov parameters*, DOI `10.1109/TAC.1973.1100208`, is the nearest direct comparator. Useful computationally, but not a strong theorem-level contribution. |
| Stratified classification of all nonminimal killed-reset augmentations | 2 | 3 | 4 | The manuscript's scalar Hankel rank supplies the minimal core, but positivity-preserving extension data are not classified. Nearest work: Ryden (1996), DOI `10.2307/3215346`, and O'Cinneide's phase-type nonuniqueness/invariant-polytope work, DOI `10.1080/15326348908807108`. Missing ingredient: a Markovian normal form across unreachable/unobservable strata. |
| Graph-uniform identifiability dichotomy for boundary zero patterns | 1 | 4 | 5 | Each fixed graph is a semialgebraic orbit-intersection problem, but no graph-uniform reduction is present. Rizk--Burke--Walsh, arXiv:`1901.03849`, proves Coxian nonuniqueness, while Radulescu et al., arXiv:`2311.03593`, DOI `10.1007/s11538-024-01385-y`, treats algebraic time-to-event identifiability. Those results rule out a naive serial-versus-nonserial dichotomy. |
| Collision-rate finite-sample singularity and a sharp local minimax law | 2 | 3 | 4 | Confluent Prony gives population multiplicity, but the manuscript lacks a local experiment for splitting poles. Batenkov--Yomdin, *On the Accuracy of Solving Confluent Prony Systems*, arXiv:`1106.1137`, DOI `10.1137/110836584`, is the nearest stability result returned. A proof would require new noisy-Hankel singular asymptotics, not the present CLT alone. |
| Global topology or connectedness of \(n\)-state Markovian orbit fibres | 2 | 3 | 3 | The local positive manifold is proved, but a global similarity orbit can meet the Markov cone in multiple components. O'Cinneide's invariant-polytopes and general phase-type representation theory are the nearest comparators. The present continuity argument supplies no global path theorem. |

## Selected result

The first candidate maximizes reach times value and directly completes the
paper's physical quotient story. For
\(K=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p&0\\b&s\end{smallmatrix}\right)\), every
reset-preserving similarity is generated by
\(M_q=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}q&1-q\\0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\).
Writing \(q_*=\Gamma/\kappa_{\mathrm r}\), the transformed upper-right entry
is \(b(1-q)(q-q_*)/q\). The lower-left entry forces \(q>0\), so Markov
nonnegativity is equivalent to \(q\) lying between \(1\) and \(q_*\). The two
endpoints are exactly the original and rate-swapped physical kernels; strict
interior points are positive nontriangular kernels. At equal rates \(q_*=1\),
the full two-state fibre collapses to one point.

- arXiv returned 20 records for `phase-type AND identifiability`, led by
  arXiv:`2605.15395` and arXiv:`1901.03849`; the exact killed-reset searches in
  the earlier ledger returned zero.
- Crossref confirmed the exact MAP2, weak-equivalence, phase-type, algebraic
  time-to-event, and Markov-parameter records and DOIs cited above.
- Semantic Scholar resolved DOI `10.1239/jap/1285335400`, returned its abstract
  and citation graph, and described conditions for distinct MAP2 parameter
  sets with identical stationary observable laws. Subsequent batch requests
  were rate-limited with HTTP 429.
- zbMATH returned nine records for `phase-type identifiability`, including
  Ryden (1996), Ramirez-Cobo--Lillo--Wiper (2010), Ramirez-Cobo--Lillo (2012),
  and recent phase-type identifiability applications; it returned the Coxian
  and algebraic time-to-event comparators separately.


 succeeded in 158ms:
# Feasibility note: n-state killed-reset D-MAP identifiability

## Decision

A universal labelled-kernel inverse is false, but a sharp minimal-class
dichotomy is attainable. Deterministic reset reduces the visible law to the
scalar Palm tail S_k = e_n^T K^k 1. Standard minimal realization theory says
that two rank-n representations of this sequence differ by a unique
similarity preserving 1 and e_n^T. Therefore a declared subclass is
identifiable, up to its allowed state relabellings, exactly when the
intersection of each such similarity orbit with the subclass's nonnegative
substochastic cone has one declared structural-equivalence class.

This orbit--cone criterion is necessary and sufficient, but its realization
input is established theory rather than a new general phase-type theorem.
The physical two-state orbit intersection can now be evaluated completely.
With \(q_*=\Gamma/\kappa_{\mathrm r}\), it is the closed arc generated by
\(M_q=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}q&1-q\\0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]\) for
\(q\) between \(1\) and \(q_*\). Its endpoints
are the two rate-swapped physical kernels, all strict interior points are
positive nontriangular kernels, and the arc collapses to a singleton exactly
when the rates coincide. The other model-specific conclusions locate two
opposite higher-state classes:

- every minimal interior point of the unrestricted killed-reset cone has a
  continuum visible fibre;
- the pure serial absorption subclass identifies the unordered rate multiset
  for all positive rates, including repeated rates.

Pole collision is therefore not the identifiability boundary. In the serial
model it produces confluent Prony terms and singular finite-sample root
coordinates, while algebraic multiplicity still identifies the repeated rate
at population level. The boundary is the Markovian orbit fibre.

## Scope

The theorem assumes minimal/Hankel rank n. Lower-rank nominal n-state
representations require a separate stratified augmentation theory. For an
arbitrary fixed nonserial zero pattern, deciding the orbit--cone intersection
remains a graph-specific semialgebraic problem. These limitations are explicit
open interfaces in the manuscript.

## Machine evidence

verify_nstate_identifiability.py evaluates representative n=2,3,4 serial
models, including repeated sampled poles. It computes Palm tails and stationary
click moments, reconstructs the order-n Hankel recurrence, verifies full
Hankel rank, checks permutation invariance, runs deterministic fibre searches,
and constructs distinct reset-preserving similarity equivalents with identical
visible tails. It also evaluates the exact physical two-state fibre arc,
checks its swapped endpoints and strict interior positivity, and verifies full
Palm-tail invariance. The legacy aggregate output is
`artifacts/verify_nstate_identifiability_output.txt`; the new focused output is
`artifacts/verify_two_state_fibre_output.txt`.

Exact citations, arXiv API queries, DOI checks, and the restricted novelty
claim are recorded in artifacts/literature_check.md.

Thus \(\|\Psi(k,\ell)\|_{\ell^2}\le B_\lambda\) for every pair.  Since every
\(\phi_j\) is double centred under \(g\),
\[
\begin{split}
 m_N:=\EE_{g_N\otimes g_N}\Psi

This displays, rather than assumes, the cancellation of the two first-order
marginal terms.

Fix \(J\).  The first \(J\) coordinates of
\(\Psi(G_i,G_{i+1})-m_N\) form a uniformly bounded one-dependent triangular
array.  Its lag-zero covariance tends to
\(\operatorname{diag}(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_J)\), while its two lag-one
covariances tend to zero: at \(g\) they vanish exactly by double centring, and
\textup{(U1)} controls the perturbation.  The fixed-dimensional
one-dependent triangular-array CLT therefore gives
\[
 (Z_{1,N},\ldots,Z_{J,N})
 \Longrightarrow(\zeta_1,\ldots,\zeta_J)
\tag{U4}
\]
uniformly under \textup{(T)}.  For completeness, uniformity follows by the
subsequence criterion: from every sequence satisfying \textup{(T)}, extract
a convergent subsequence of
\(\sqrt N(\sigma_N-\sigma_0)\); boundedness gives Lindeberg, \textup{(U1)}
gives the covariance limit, and the usual even--odd blocking proves the
one-dependent CLT.  The uniform renewal law of large numbers replaces the
deterministic number of pairs by \(K_N-1\), and
\(\widehat\mu_N-\mu_N=o_P(1)\).  These replacements are uniform because the
common exponential moment controls both endpoint cycles and the renewal-count
deviation.  The mean contribution in \textup{(U3)} is
\(O(\sqrt N\,\|m_N\|_{\ell^2})=o(1)\).

The passage from fixed \(J\) to the full statistic is also uniform.  Put
\[
 L_J=\sum_{j>J}\lambda_j\|\phi_j\|_\infty^2\longrightarrow0 .
\]
The even--odd maximal inequality for the centred one-dependent tail feature,
followed by the renewal-count bound \(K_N\le N\), gives
\[
 \sup_{\sigma_N:\,\textup{(T)}}
 \EE_{\sigma_N}\!\left[
 \sum_{j>J}\lambda_jZ_{j,N}^2;\,
 K_N\ge2\right]
 \le C L_J+o(1).
\tag{U5}
\]
Here the \(o(1)\) contains the squared mean from \textup{(U3)} and the event
on which \(\widehat\mu_N\) is outside a fixed compact interval.  The latter
event is exponentially small by the common exponential renewal bound; on its
complement \(\widehat\mu_N\) is uniformly bounded.  This proves
\textup{(U5)} without treating the random number of overlapping pairs as a
conditionally binomial count.

For the limit variable,
\[
 \EE\sum_{j>J}\lambda_j\zeta_j^2
 =\sum_{j>J}\lambda_j\longrightarrow0.
\tag{U6}
\]
Equations \textup{(U4)}--\textup{(U6)} and Markov's inequality give uniform
bounded-Lipschitz convergence of \(Q_N\) to
\(Q_0=\sum_j\lambda_j\zeta_j^2\).  The law of \(Q_0\) is continuous because
\(\lambda_1>0\), so evaluation at \(c_{\alpha,\lambda}\) proves
\textup{(U)}.  Finally,
\[
 (p_N+s_N,p_Ns_N)
 =(2z,z^2-h^2N^{-1/2}),
\]
so the stated \(N^{-1/4}\) root-splitting sequences satisfy \textup{(T)}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Exchange-point Helmert coupling bracket]
\label{prop:helmert-growing-layer-bracket-main}
Retain the exchange-point notation of
\Cref{thm:complete-markov-palm-tangent-omnibus-main}, put
$p_0=e^{-x_0}$, and write
\[
 S_j=\PP_g(G\geq j)=p_0^j(1+x_0j),\qquad j\geq0.
\]
For $j\geq0$ define the weighted Helmert contrast
\[
 e_j(k)=
 \sqrt{\frac{S_{j+1}}{g_jS_j}}\,\mathbf1_{\{k=j\}}
 -\sqrt{\frac{g_j}{S_jS_{j+1}}}\,\mathbf1_{\{k\geq j+1\}}.
 \tag{V}
\]
Thus $e_0$ is the contrast used in
\Cref{thm:complete-markov-palm-tangent-omnibus-main}, and
$e_0,e_1,\ldots$ is an orthonormal basis of $L^2_0(g)$.  For $J\geq1$ let
\[
 \boldsymbol\Phi_J=
 \bigl(e_a\otimes e_b:0\leq a,b\leq J,\ (a,b)\neq(0,0)\bigr),
 \qquad d_J=(J+1)^2-1,
 \tag{W}
\]
in any fixed order, and let $\mathbf Z_{J,N}$ be the corresponding vector of
scores in \textup{(Q)}, with the basis in \textup{(V)} fixed at $\theta_0$.
Set $n_N=N/\mu$.

If $J_N\to\infty$ and
\[
 \frac{d_{J_N}}{\sqrt{n_N}\,S_{J_N}}\longrightarrow0,
 \tag{$X_{\rm suff}$}
\]
then, on extensions of the underlying probability spaces, there are
$\boldsymbol\xi_N\sim\mathcal N(0,I_{d_{J_N}})$ such that
\[
 \|\mathbf Z_{J_N,N}-\boldsymbol\xi_N\|_2=o_{P_0}(1).
 \tag{Y}
\]
This holds at the fixed exchange point $\theta_0$ and uniformly along the local
sampled-counter null sequences in \textup{(T)}.  Conversely, \textup{(Y)} at
$\theta_0$ implies the necessary condition
\[
 n_NS_{J_N}^2\longrightarrow\infty.
 \tag{$X_{\rm nec}$}
\]
No sufficiency claim under \textup{($X_{\rm nec}$)} alone is made.  Closing the
gap between \textup{($X_{\rm suff}$)} and \textup{($X_{\rm nec}$)} is an open
interface: the general Euclidean Yurinskii bound used here retains an ambient
dimension factor.

Replacing the deterministic cycle count by the
calendar stopping index and replacing $\mu$ by $\widehat\mu_N$ contribute,
respectively,
\[
 O_P(J_NN^{-1/4})\quad\hbox{and}\quad O_P(J_NN^{-1/2})
 \tag{Z}
\]
in Euclidean norm.  The local null covariance perturbation contributes
$O_P(J_N^3N^{-1/2})$; all three terms are $o_P(1)$ under
\textup{($X_{\rm suff}$)}.

The necessity in \textup{($X_{\rm nec}$)} already follows from the fact that a standard
Gaussian limit for the moving corner coordinate
$e_{J_N}\otimes e_{J_N}$ requires
\[
 n_NS_{J_N}^2\longrightarrow\infty.
 \tag{AA}
\]
More strongly, if some $r_N<J_N$ satisfies
$n_NS_{r_N}^2\to0$ and $J_N-r_N\asymp J_N$, then, with probability tending
to one, every score in the block
\[
 \{e_a\otimes e_b:r_N\leq a,b\leq J_N\}
 \tag{AB}
\]
is zero.  The corresponding standard Gaussian block has
$(J_N-r_N+1)^2$ degrees of freedom, so neither an unweighted chi-square
approximation for that block nor an $o_P(1)$ Euclidean coupling can hold.

Both sides of the bracket have first-order layer constant $(2x_0)^{-1}$, but
their second-order terms differ.  For any $\omega_N\to\infty$, a sufficient
region is
\[
 J_N\leq
 \frac{\log n_N-2\log\log n_N-\omega_N}{2x_0},
 \tag{AC$_{\rm suff}$}
\]
whereas coupling requires
\[
 J_N\leq
 \frac{\log n_N+2\log\log n_N-\omega_N}{2x_0}
 \tag{AC$_{\rm nec}$}
\]
for some diverging $\omega_N$.
If
$2x_0J_N=\log n_N+2\log\log n_N-c+o(1)$ for a fixed $c\in\mathbb R$, then
$n_NS_{J_N}^2\to e^c/4$, so \textup{(Y)} fails.  The previously verified
sufficient region is therefore separated from the necessary boundary by a
$4\log\log n_N$ window.  No assertion of sharpness or Le Cam equivalence is
made.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The equal-rate dictionary gives the displayed formula for $S_j$.
The two values in \textup{(V)} have weighted mean zero and weighted squared
norm one.  Nested supports then give
$\langle e_j,e_k\rangle_{L^2(g)}=0$ for $j\neq k$; completeness follows by
successively separating the atoms $0,1,\ldots$ from the remaining tail.
Since $S_{j+1}/S_j\to p_0$ and $g_j/S_j\to1-p_0$,
\[
 \|e_j\|_\infty\asymp S_j^{-1/2},\qquad
 \max_{a,b\leq J}\|e_a\otimes e_b\|_\infty\asymp S_J^{-1}.
 \tag{1}
\]

For the deterministic-cycle argument, let
$K_J(k)=\sum_{a=0}^Je_a(k)^2$.  Adding the constant function to the Helmert
contrasts gives the projection onto the partition
$\{0\},\ldots,\{J\},\{J+1,J+2,\ldots\}$; hence
\[
 1+K_J(k)=
 \begin{cases}
  g_k^{-1},&0\leq k\leq J,\\
  S_{J+1}^{-1},&k\geq J+1.
 \end{cases}
 \tag{2}
\]
The equal-rate exponential tail and \textup{(2)} imply
\[
 \EE K_J(G)^{3/2}\leq C S_J^{-1/2},\qquad
 \EE K_J(G)^2\leq C S_J^{-1}.
 \tag{3}
\]
Thus, for the vector $\boldsymbol\Phi_J(G_i,G_{i+1})$, independence of the
two gaps yields
\[
 \EE\|\boldsymbol\Phi_J(G_i,G_{i+1})\|_2^3\leq C S_J^{-1}.
 \tag{4}
\]
We now prove the fixed exchange-point sufficiency.  Write $m=J+1$, let
$P_J:\mathbb R^{m^2}\to\mathbb R^{d_J}$ delete the $e_0\otimes e_0$
coordinate, and set
\[
 h_J(G)=(e_0(G),\ldots,e_J(G))^\top,
 \qquad
 Y_{i,J}=P_J\{h_J(G_i)\otimes h_J(G_{i+1})\}.
 \tag{B1}
\]
Then $\EE Y_{i,J}=0$ and
$\EE(Y_{i,J}Y_{i,J}^\top)=I_{d_J}$.  Moreover, double centring gives, for
all retained coordinates,
\[
 \EE\{e_a(G_i)e_b(G_{i+1})e_c(G_{i+1})e_d(G_{i+2})\}=0.
 \tag{B2}
\]
Thus distinct $Y_{i,J}$ are uncorrelated, and they are independent when their
indices differ by more than one.

Put $n=\lfloor n_N\rfloor$ and
$L=\lceil d_J^2+\log n\rceil$.  Partition the edge indices into consecutive
blocks of length at most $L$, deleting one connecting edge between successive
blocks.  The retained block sums are independent because their underlying
gap sets are disjoint.  If $I$ and $D$ denote the retained and deleted edge
sets, respectively, then
\[

exec
"C:\\Windows\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command '$lines=Get-Content -Encoding UTF8 '"'main.tex'; "'$lines[2296..2475]' in D:\omega\automath\papers\publication\2026_detector_shells_click_record_kms_jphyscomm
 succeeded in 174ms:
 r:=|D|\leq \frac{n}{L+1}+L+1,
 \qquad
 \EE\left\|\frac1{\sqrt n}\sum_{i\in D}Y_{i,J}\right\|_2^2
 =\frac{rd_J}{n}
 \leq C\left(\frac{d_J}{L}+\frac{Ld_J}{n}\right)=o(1).
 \tag{B3}
\]
For a retained block $B$, split its edges by parity.  Each parity class is
independent, so the Hilbert-space Rosenthal--Burkholder inequality
\cite{Pinelis1994OptimalMartingaleBounds}, together with \textup{(4)}, yields
\[
 \EE\left\|\sum_{i\in B}Y_{i,J}\right\|_2^3
 \leq C\{(Ld_J)^{3/2}+L/S_J\}.
 \tag{B4}
\]
Let $\mathcal B$ be the retained blocks, put
$X_B=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i\in B}Y_{i,J}$, and let
$V_B=(|B|/n)I_{d_J}$ be its covariance.  For independent
$Z_B\sim\mathcal N(0,I_{d_J})$, the second-order moment quantity in
CMU's Corollary~2.3 is
\[
 \widetilde\beta_N
 :=\sum_{B\in\mathcal B}\EE\{\|X_B\|_2^3
       +\|V_B^{1/2}Z_B\|_2^3\}
 \leq C\left\{
  \frac{\sqrt L\,d_J^{3/2}}{\sqrt n}
  +\frac1{\sqrt n\,S_J}
 \right\}.
 \tag{B5}
\]
Here the first summand follows from \textup{(B4)}.  For the Gaussian summand,
$\EE\|Z_B\|_2^3\leq C d_J^{3/2}$ and
$\sum_{B\in\mathcal B}|B|^{3/2}\leq\sqrt L\,n$, giving the same first
order.  This Gaussian comparison contribution is part of the cited theorem
and cannot be omitted.

We use the independent-vector Euclidean Yurinskii theorem in CMU's
Corollary~2.3 \cite{CattaneoMasiniUnderwood2025Yurinskii}.
Since $\phi_2(d_J)^2=2d_J$, it gives for every $\eta>0$ a coupling of the retained sum
$A=n^{-1/2}\sum_{i\in I}Y_{i,J}$ to
$T\sim\mathcal N(0,cI_{d_J})$, $c=|I|/n$, satisfying
\[
 \PP(\|A-T\|_2>\eta)
 \leq24\left(\frac{2d_J\widetilde\beta_N}{\eta^3}\right)^{1/3}.
 \tag{B6}
\]
Under \textup{($X_{\rm suff}$)}, $J=O(\log n)$ and
\[
 d_J\widetilde\beta_N
 \leq C\left\{
 \frac{\sqrt L\,d_J^{5/2}}{\sqrt n}
 +\frac{d_J}{\sqrt n\,S_J}\right\}=o(1);
\]
the first term is $O((\log n)^7/\sqrt n)$ and the second is exactly the
condition \textup{($X_{\rm suff}$)}, up to replacing $n_N$ by $n$.
Put $q_N=2d_J\widetilde\beta_N$ and choose the coupling in \textup{(B6)}
with $\eta_N=q_N^{1/6}$.  Then $\eta_N\to0$ and
\[
 \PP(\|A-T\|_2>\eta_N)\leq24q_N^{1/6}\longrightarrow0.
\]
This explicit diagonal choice is needed because the coupling variable in the
cited theorem depends on the requested tolerance; it proves
$\|A-T\|_2=o_P(1)$.  The covariance is exactly $cI_{d_J}$ by
\textup{(B2)}.  If $Z\sim\mathcal N(0,I_{d_J})$ is independent of $T$, then
\[
 \xi=T+\sqrt{1-c}\,Z\sim\mathcal N(0,I_{d_J}),
 \qquad
 \EE\|\xi-T\|_2^2=(1-c)d_J=\frac{rd_J}{n}=o(1).
 \tag{B7}
\]
Combining \textup{(B3)}--\textup{(B7)} proves the deterministic-cycle
exchange-point coupling under \textup{($X_{\rm suff}$)}.

For completeness, the uniform local-null result under
\textup{($X_{\rm suff}$)} follows by retaining the direct martingale argument.
The vectors in \textup{(B1)} are martingale differences for the filtration
ending at $G_{i+1}$.  If $V_i$ denotes the conditional covariance of the
$i$th normalized increment, then its block form and \textup{(2)} give
\[
 \EE\left\|\sum_{i\leq n_N}V_i-I_{d_J}\right\|_{\rm op}
 \leq
 \left\{\EE\left\|\sum_{i\leq n_N}V_i-I_{d_J}\right\|_{\rm F}^2\right\}^{1/2}
 \leq C\sqrt{\frac{J}{n_NS_J}}.
 \tag{5}
\]
Here the last inequality uses
$\|e(G)e(G)^\top\|_{\rm F}^2=K_J(G)^2$ and the second bound in
\textup{(3)}.  The ordinary martingale Yurinskii quantity is
$O((\sqrt{n_N}S_J)^{-1})$ by \textup{(4)}.  Since the Euclidean Gaussian
norm factor is of order $\sqrt{d_J}$, condition
\textup{($X_{\rm suff}$)} makes both the Yurinskii term
$d_J/(\sqrt{n_N}S_J)$ and the conditional-covariance term
$d_J\sqrt{J/(n_NS_J)}$ tend to zero.  Therefore
CMU's Corollary~2.2
\cite{CattaneoMasiniUnderwood2025Yurinskii} gives \textup{(Y)} under
\textup{($X_{\rm suff}$)}, uniformly after the perturbation below.  Choosing
the coupling tolerance by the same deterministic diagonal argument makes this
a single uniform $o_P(1)$ coupling sequence.

The renewal central limit theorem gives
$K_N-n_N=O_P(\sqrt N)$.  A Hilbert-space martingale maximal inequality over
that random terminal window gives squared Euclidean error
$O_P(d_J/\sqrt N)$ after normalization, which is the square of the first
order in
\textup{(Z)}.  The usual root-$N$ consistency of $\widehat\mu_N$ gives the
second.  It remains to make the triangular local-null comparison explicit.
Let $\mathcal P_J$ be the partition in \textup{(2)} and let
$g_N=g_{\sigma_N}$.  Analytic differentiation of the exact sampled-counter
atom and tail formulae at the exchange point gives, uniformly under
\textup{(T)} and for $J=O(\log N)$,
\[
 \max_{C\in\mathcal P_J}
 \left|\frac{g_N(C)}{g(C)}-1\right|
 \leq C N^{-1/2}(1+J^2).
 \tag{6}
\]
For the singleton cells this follows from the density-ratio derivatives,
which are $O(1+k^2)$ for $k\leq J$; for the final tail cell it follows by
differentiating the closed tail formula before taking the ratio.  Every
coordinate in \textup{(W)} is constant on each product cell of
$\mathcal P_J\otimes\mathcal P_J$.  Consequently \textup{(6)} implies
\[
 \|\Sigma_{J,N}-I_{d_J}\|_{\rm op}=O(J^2N^{-1/2}),
 \tag{7}
\]
where $\Sigma_{J,N}$ is the score covariance under $g_N$.  Moreover, if
$u_{a,N}=\EE_{g_N}e_a$, double centring under $g$ gives
\[
 \left\|(u_{a,N}u_{b,N})_{(a,b)\neq(0,0)}\right\|_2
 \leq\sum_{a=0}^J u_{a,N}^2=O(N^{-1}),
 \tag{8}
\]
so the normalized score mean is $O(N^{-1/2})$.  Coupling
$\Sigma_{J,N}^{1/2}\boldsymbol\xi_N$ and $\boldsymbol\xi_N$ with the same
standard Gaussian vector, \textup{(7)} and the square-root perturbation
bound give
\[
 \|(\Sigma_{J,N}^{1/2}-I)\boldsymbol\xi_N\|_2
 =O_P(\sqrt{d_J}J^2N^{-1/2})=O_P(J^3N^{-1/2}).
 \tag{9}
\]
Condition \textup{($X_{\rm suff}$)} implies $J_N=O(\log N)$.  Hence the
deterministic-cycle block coupling and the two stopping terms prove
\textup{(Y)} at the exchange point, whereas \textup{(6)}--\textup{(9)} and
the martingale coupling prove the uniform local-null statement, both under
\textup{($X_{\rm suff}$)}.

The corner contrast is nonzero exactly when both gaps are at least $J$.
On that event its magnitude is bounded above and below by constant multiples
of $S_J^{-1}$, by \textup{(1)}.  Its triangular-array Lindeberg condition is
therefore exactly $\sqrt{n_N}S_{J_N}\to\infty$, which is \textup{(AA)}.
If this fails along a subsequence, the number of nonzero corner summands has
a nonvanishing atom at zero (and is asymptotically Poisson whenever
$n_NS_{J_N}^2$ has a finite positive limit), excluding a standard normal
limit.  For \textup{(AB)}, the expected number of adjacent pairs with both
gaps at least $r_N$ is $O(n_NS_{r_N}^2)=o(1)$.  Markov's inequality proves
the zero-block assertion, and the Gaussian comparison follows from its
chi-square block energy.

Finally,
\[
 \log(n_NS_{J_N}^2)
 =\log n_N-2x_0J_N+2\log(1+x_0J_N).
 \tag{10}
\]
If \textup{($X_{\rm nec}$)} is near its maximal band, then
$J_N\asymp\log n_N$ and
$2\log(1+x_0J_N)=2\log\log n_N-2\log2+o(1)$.
This proves the necessary region \textup{(AC$_{\rm nec}$)}.  Similarly,
\[
 \log\frac{d_{J_N}}{\sqrt{n_N}S_{J_N}}
 =\log d_{J_N}-\tfrac12\log n_N+x_0J_N
   -\log(1+x_0J_N),
\]
which gives \textup{(AC$_{\rm suff}$)}.  Under the critical scaling displayed
in the proposition, \textup{(10)} converges to $c-2\log2$, which gives
$n_NS_{J_N}^2\to e^c/4$.  The two calculations leave the stated
$4\log\log n_N$ window unresolved.
\end{proof}

   -\log(1+x_0J_N),
\]
which gives \textup{(AC$_{\rm suff}$)}.  Under the critical scaling displayed
in the proposition, \textup{(10)} converges to $c-2\log2$, which gives
$n_NS_{J_N}^2\to e^c/4$.  The two calculations leave the stated
$4\log\log n_N$ window unresolved.
\end{proof}

\begin{paperthm}[Fixed-setting retained-record quotient delta method]\label{thm:finite-record-quotient-delta-main}
Assume the hypotheses of Theorem~C and suppose the population inverse lies in
the stable quotient chart
\[
r_0>0,\qquad \Delta_{\rm inv}=r_1-r_0^2\ne0 .
\]
For the quotient map \(\Phi\) in Theorem~B,
\[
\sqrt N\{\Phi(\Rinch_N^{\rm cyc})-\Phi(\Rinc)\}
\Rightarrow
\mathcal N(0,\Sigma_\sigma),
\qquad
\Sigma_\sigma=D\Phi(\Rinc)\Sigma_rD\Phi(\Rinc)^\top .
\]
For the constrained D-MAP population, \(\Phi(\Rinc)=(p+s,ps)\).  If
\[
\Delta_\sigma=\Phi_1(\Rinc)^2-4\Phi_2(\Rinc)=(p-s)^2>0,
\]
let \(z_-<z_+\) be the sorted roots and let
\(\widehat z_{-,N}<\widehat z_{+,N}\) be the correspondingly sorted roots
formed from \(\Phi(\Rinch_N^{\rm cyc})\).  This ordering is visible and
requires no external physical label.  With
\[
J_{\rm root}=
\begin{pmatrix}
\dfrac{z_-}{z_--z_+}&-\dfrac1{z_--z_+}\\[6pt]
\dfrac{z_+}{z_+-z_-}&-\dfrac1{z_+-z_-}
\end{pmatrix},
\]
the label-free root CLT is
\[
\sqrt N
\begin{pmatrix}\widehat z_{-,N}-z_-\\ \widehat z_{+,N}-z_+\end{pmatrix}
\Rightarrow
\mathcal N\!\left(0,J_{\rm root}\Sigma_\sigma J_{\rm root}^{\top}\right).
\]
For known design interval \(\Delta\tau\), define the root-ordered rates
\[
\vartheta=
\begin{pmatrix}-\log z_-/\Delta\tau\\-\log z_+/\Delta\tau\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
\widehat\vartheta_N=
\begin{pmatrix}-\log\widehat z_{-,N}/\Delta\tau\\
-\log\widehat z_{+,N}/\Delta\tau\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Then
\[
\sqrt N(\widehat\vartheta_N-\vartheta)
\Rightarrow\mathcal N(0,J_{\rm rate}\Sigma_\sigma J_{\rm rate}^{\top}),
\qquad
J_{\rm rate}=
\begin{pmatrix}-(\Delta\tau z_-)^{-1}&0\\
0&-(\Delta\tau z_+)^{-1}\end{pmatrix}J_{\rm root}.
\]
Thus the roots identify the unordered survival-factor set \(\{p,s\}\) and
the displayed root ordering gives a canonical, label-free version of the
unordered numerical rate set
\[
\left\{-\frac{\log p}{\Delta\tau},-\frac{\log s}{\Delta\tau}\right\}.
\]
If \(\Delta\tau\) is not supplied as a known design interval, the theorem
identifies only the dimensionless unordered product set
\[
\{-\log p,-\log s\}
=\{\Gamma\Delta\tau,\kappa_{\mathrm r}\Delta\tau\},
\]
not numerical rates per unit time.
Only the assignment of the two numerical entries to the physical names
\(\Gamma\) and \(\kappa_{\mathrm r}\) requires the side-data branch
convention.
\end{paperthm}

\begin{maintheorem}[Compact-uniform singular exchange experiment]
\label{thm:singular-exchange-limit-experiment-main}
Let \(\mathcal K\Subset(0,1)\) and \(H<\infty\).  Denote by
\(P_{p,s}^{(N)}\) the law of the stationary visible record
\((A_1,\ldots,A_N)\) under the sampled-counter model with survival factors
\((p,s)\).  For \(z\in\mathcal K\), \(|h|\le H\), and all sufficiently large
\(N\), put
\[
 p_{N,z,h}=z+hN^{-1/4},\qquad
 s_{N,z,h}=z-hN^{-1/4},\qquad
 P_{N,z,h}=P_{p_{N,z,h},s_{N,z,h}}^{(N)}.
\tag{SE1}
\]
At the diagonal let
\[
 D_z(k)=1-z+\log z\{kz-k+z\},\qquad
 g_z(k)=z^kD_z(k),\qquad k\ge0,
\tag{SE2}
\]
and define
\[
\begin{split}
B_z(k)=\frac{z^{k-2}}6\bigl[&
k^3(z-1)\log z+3k^2\{\log z+1-z\}\\
&-3kz\log z-6kz-2z\log z-3z\bigr],
\qquad b_z(k)=\frac{B_z(k)}{g_z(k)} .
\end{split}
\tag{SE3}
\]
Then \(g_z\) is the diagonal Palm gap mass and
\[
 g_{z+d,z-d}(k)=g_z(k)+d^2B_z(k)+O(d^4)
\tag{SE4}
\]
for every \(k\).  Moreover,
\[
 \sum_{k\ge0}g_z(k)b_z(k)=0,\qquad
 \mu_z=\sum_{k\ge0}(k+1)g_z(k)
 =\frac{1-z-z\log z}{(1-z)^2},
\tag{SE5}
\]
and
\[
 \mathcal I(z)=\frac1{\mu_z}
 \sum_{k\ge0}g_z(k)b_z(k)^2
 =\frac1{\mu_z}\sum_{k\ge0}\frac{B_z(k)^2}{g_z(k)}
\tag{SE6}
\]
is finite, strictly positive, and continuous on \((0,1)\).

Delete the two incomplete endpoint cycles and write the complete gaps as
\(G_{1,N},\ldots,G_{K_N,N}\).  Set
\[
 \Delta_{N,z}=\frac1{\sqrt N}
 \sum_{j=1}^{K_N}b_z(G_{j,N}).
\tag{SE7}
\]
Uniformly for \(z\in\mathcal K\),
\[
 \Delta_{N,z}\ \Longrightarrow\
 \mathcal N\{0,\mathcal I(z)\}
\tag{SE8}
\]
in bounded-Lipschitz distance under \(P_{N,z,0}\).  More precisely, for every
\(\varepsilon>0\),
\[
\sup_{\substack{z\in\mathcal K\\ |h|\le H}}
P_{N,z,0}\!\left(
\left|
\log\frac{dP_{N,z,h}}{dP_{N,z,0}}
-h^2\Delta_{N,z}
+\frac12h^4\mathcal I(z)
\right|>\varepsilon
\right)\longrightarrow0 .
\tag{SE9}
\]
Consequently the likelihood-ratio processes, indexed by \(|h|\le H\),
converge uniformly over \(z\in\mathcal K\) to the scalar Gaussian experiments
\[
 X_z\sim\mathcal N\!\left(h^2\sqrt{\mathcal I(z)},1\right),
\qquad |h|\le H.
\tag{SE10}
\]
Equivalently, under \(P_{N,z,h}\),
\[
 \Delta_{N,z}\Longrightarrow
 \mathcal N\{h^2\mathcal I(z),\mathcal I(z)\}
\tag{SE11}
\]
uniformly over \(z\in\mathcal K\) and bounded \(h\).
The limit identifies \(h^2\), not the sign of \(h\).  This is the complete
singular experiment for the specified path; it is not an ordered hidden-label
experiment.
\end{maintheorem}

\begin{proof}
Write \(x=-\log p\), \(y=-\log s\).  The Palm gap formula in Theorem~A is
\[
 g_{p,s}(k)
 =\frac{y(1-p)p^k-x(1-s)s^k}{y-x},
\tag{SE12}
\]
with its continuous value when \(p=s\).  Substitute \(p=z+d\) and \(s=z-d\)
and expand the even analytic function of \(d\).  The constant and quadratic
coefficients are exactly \textup{(SE2)} and \textup{(SE3)}, proving
\textup{(SE4)}.  The diagonal survival tail is
\[
 S_z(k)=z^k(1-k\log z).
\]
Thus \(g_z(k)=S_z(k)-S_z(k+1)\), which proves the first identity in
\textup{(SE2)} and, by summing the tails, the formula for \(\mu_z\).
Differentiating \(\sum_kg_{z+d,z-d}(k)=1\) at order \(d^2\) gives
\(\sum_kB_z(k)=0\), hence the centring in \textup{(SE5)}.

On every \(\mathcal K\Subset(0,1)\), the masses in \textup{(SE12)} and all
their derivatives through any fixed order have a common
\(C(1+k)^m r^k\) envelope for some \(r<1\).  Termwise differentiation and
summation are therefore valid uniformly on \(\mathcal K\).  Formula
\textup{(SE3)} gives \(|b_z(k)|\le C_{\mathcal K}(1+k)^2\), so
\textup{(SE6)} is finite and continuous.  It is positive: if it vanished,
then \(B_z(k)=0\) for every \(k\).  But
\[
 B_z(0)=-\frac{2\log z+3}{6z};
\]
its only zero is \(z=e^{-3/2}\), and at that point the numerator in
\textup{(SE3)} for \(k=1\) equals \(-6z\), so \(B_z(1)\ne0\).

Put \(q=d^2\).  Uniform analytic differentiation in the symmetric coordinate
\((p+s,ps)=(2z,z^2-q)\) gives
\[
 g_{z+\sqrt q,z-\sqrt q}(k)
 =g_z(k)\{1+qb_z(k)+q^2c_z(k)+q^3R_{z,q}(k)\},
\tag{SE13}
\]
where \(\sum_kg_z(k)c_z(k)=0\) and \(c_z,R_{z,q}\) have common polynomial
envelopes on \(\mathcal K\) for \(q\) in a fixed right neighbourhood of zero.
The same statement follows directly from \textup{(SE12)}.  Uniform
exponential gap tails imply
\[
 \max_{1\le j\le K_N}G_{j,N}=O_{P_{N,z,0}}(\log N)
\tag{SE14}
\]
uniformly in \(z\in\mathcal K\).  Taylor expansion of the logarithm in
\textup{(SE13)}, on the event in \textup{(SE14)}, therefore gives uniformly
for \(|h|\le H\), with \(q=h^2N^{-1/2}\),
\[
\begin{split}
\sum_{j=1}^{K_N}\log
\frac{g_{z+\sqrt q,z-\sqrt q}(G_{j,N})}{g_z(G_{j,N})}
={}&h^2\Delta_{N,z}\\
&+\frac{h^4}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{K_N}
\{c_z(G_{j,N})-\tfrac12b_z(G_{j,N})^2\}+o_P(1).
\end{split}
\tag{SE15}
\]

Under the diagonal law the complete Palm gaps are consecutive i.i.d.
variables with mass \(g_z\), and the renewal count satisfies
\(K_N/N\to1/\mu_z\).  The uniform regenerative law of large numbers applied
to the two polynomially bounded rewards in \textup{(SE15)} makes its second
line converge to \(-h^4\mathcal I(z)/2\), uniformly on
\(\mathcal K\times[-H,H]\).  The uniform regenerative central limit theorem
applied to \(b_z\) proves \textup{(SE8)}.  Both results can also be obtained by
the subsequence criterion: every sequence \(z_N\in\mathcal K\) has a
convergent subsequence, and the common exponential envelope supplies
Lindeberg, renewal-count replacement, and uniform integrability along that
subsequence.

It remains to compare the product of complete-gap likelihoods with the
stationary \(N\)-bin likelihood.  Their ratio contains only the left
equilibrium-delay factor, the right censored-tail factor, and the normalizing
cycle mean.  Their logarithmic \(q\)-derivatives have polynomial envelopes in
the two endpoint lengths.  Those lengths are \(O_P(\log N)\) uniformly by the
same exponential-tail bound, so their total log-likelihood contribution is
\(O_P(N^{-1/2}\operatorname{polylog}N)=o_P(1)\).  This proves
\textup{(SE9)}.

The right side of \textup{(SE9)} is a continuous function of \(h\) and the
remainder is uniform on the compact index set.  Hence the likelihood-ratio
processes converge in \(C([-H,H])\) to
\[
 h\longmapsto
 \exp\{h^2\sqrt{\mathcal I(z)}\,Z-\tfrac12h^4\mathcal I(z)\},
 \qquad Z\sim\mathcal N(0,1),
\]
which is \textup{(SE10)}.  Le Cam's third lemma gives \textup{(SE11)}.
Finally,
\[
\sqrt N\{(p_{N,z,h}+s_{N,z,h},p_{N,z,h}s_{N,z,h})
-(2z,z^2)\}=(0,-h^2),
\]
which explains both the \(N^{-1/4}\) scale and the loss of the sign.  General
locally conic and loss-of-identifiability asymptotics are established in
\cite{DacunhaCastelleGassiat1997LocallyConic,
LiuShao2003LossIdentifiability}; the calculation above is the
compact-uniform stationary-renewal specialization.
\end{proof}

\begin{paperthm}[Pointwise exchange-diagonal projection example]\label{thm:e-fixed-candidate-exchange-diagonal-main}
Fix
\[
z_0=\frac12,\qquad \sigma_0=(1,1/4),\qquad
H_{z_0}=\{h\in\mathbb R^2:h_2\le z_0h_1\},
\]
and let \(F=\{(\sigma_1,\sigma_2):\sigma_2\le\sigma_1^2/4\}\).  Suppose the
true population satisfies \(\sigma=\sigma_0\), the printed interval
certificate proves
\(\lambda_{\min}\{\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)\}>0.037\), and
\[
\sqrt N(\widehat\sigma_N^{\rm raw}-\sigma_0)
\Rightarrow Z\sim\mathcal N(0,\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)).
\]
Let \(\Pi_F\) be the locally unique Euclidean projection, with any fixed
measurable tie rule outside the local neighbourhood, and set
\[
\widehat\sigma_N=\Pi_F(\widehat\sigma_N^{\rm raw}),\qquad
X_N=\sqrt N(\widehat\sigma_N-\sigma_0).
\]
Then
\[
X_N\Rightarrow \Pi_{H_{z_0}}Z .
\]
This theorem is only a pointwise projection example at the preselected point
\(z_0=1/2\).  It defines no statistic for testing, no simulated or limiting
critical value, no confidence region, and no feasible or oracle coverage
claim.  Selection of \(z_0\) from the data and uniformity over \(0<z<1\) are
outside its scope.
\end{paperthm}

\begin{proof}
Near \(\sigma_0\), the boundary of \(F\) is the smooth graph
\(\sigma_2=\sigma_1^2/4\), whose tangent cone at \(\sigma_0\) is
\(H_{z_0}\).  The standard first-order metric-projection expansion at a smooth
inequality boundary is
\[
\sqrt N\{\Pi_F(\sigma_0+N^{-1/2}h_N)-\sigma_0\}
=\Pi_{H_{z_0}}h_N+o(1)
\]
for every bounded convergent sequence \(h_N\)
\cite{Shapiro1987MetricProjection}.  Tightness of the raw quotient CLT permits
the expansion on events with probability tending to one.  Slutsky's theorem
and continuity of projection onto the closed half-space give the asserted
limit.  The certificate establishes only that the covariance in this limit is
nonsingular; it is not used to calibrate a procedure.
\end{proof}

"""Independent numerical checks for the verified A8 sampled-counter results.

The manuscript contains the proofs.  This script checks the exact physical
image residual, the sharp hidden-mode bound and its diagonal extremizer, the
diagonal counterexample to the oracle's unqualified one-dependence equation,
the fast-sampling expansion, the A8-r2 joint physical-image test, and the
A8-r3 finite-range Markov-gap specification score.  It also checks the full
fixed-marginal Markov--Palm tangent projection and finite-dimensional basis
used by the A8-r4 omnibus-score result, and the compact-uniform singular
exchange expansion and information series.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import itertools
import math
from statistics import NormalDist
from typing import Iterable

import mpmath as mp
import numpy as np
from numpy.typing import ArrayLike, NDArray


FloatArray = NDArray[np.float64]


def _analytic_h_and_derivative(w: float) -> tuple[float, float]:
    """Return H(w) and H'(w) on the real analytic branch w < 1."""
    if not math.isfinite(w) or w >= 1.0:
        raise ValueError("w must be finite and less than one")
    if abs(w) < 1e-6:
        value = 1.0 - w / 3.0 - 4.0 * w**2 / 45.0 - 44.0 * w**3 / 945.0
        derivative = -1.0 / 3.0 - 8.0 * w / 45.0 - 44.0 * w**2 / 315.0
        return value, derivative
    if w > 0.0:
        root = math.sqrt(w)
        angle = math.atanh(root)
        value = root / angle
        derivative = (angle - root / (1.0 - root**2)) / (
            2.0 * root * angle**2
        )
        return value, derivative
    root = math.sqrt(-w)
    angle = math.atan(root)
    value = root / angle
    derivative = -(angle - root / (1.0 + root**2)) / (
        2.0 * root * angle**2
    )
    return value, derivative


def analytic_log_divided_difference(sigma1: float, sigma2: float) -> float:
    """Evaluate the real analytic continuation in symmetric coordinates."""
    if not (math.isfinite(sigma1) and math.isfinite(sigma2)):
        raise ValueError("symmetric coordinates must be finite")
    if sigma1 <= 0.0 or sigma2 <= 0.0:
        raise ValueError("sigma1 and sigma2 must be positive")
    w = 1.0 - 4.0 * sigma2 / sigma1**2
    h, _ = _analytic_h_and_derivative(w)
    return 0.5 * sigma1 * (1.0 - 0.5 * math.log(sigma2) * h)


def _analytic_log_divided_difference_gradient(
    sigma1: float, sigma2: float
) -> FloatArray:
    w = 1.0 - 4.0 * sigma2 / sigma1**2
    h, h_prime = _analytic_h_and_derivative(w)
    log_sigma2 = math.log(sigma2)
    dw_dsigma1 = 8.0 * sigma2 / sigma1**3
    dw_dsigma2 = -4.0 / sigma1**2
    common = 1.0 - 0.5 * log_sigma2 * h
    d_sigma1 = 0.5 * common - 0.25 * sigma1 * log_sigma2 * h_prime * dw_dsigma1
    d_sigma2 = -0.25 * sigma1 * (
        h / sigma2 + log_sigma2 * h_prime * dw_dsigma2
    )
    return np.array([d_sigma1, d_sigma2])


def constraint_jacobian(coordinates: ArrayLike) -> FloatArray:
    """Return the Jacobian of (analytic image residual, discriminant)."""
    r0, r1, r2 = np.asarray(coordinates, dtype=float)
    denominator = r1 - r0**2
    if r0 <= 0.0 or denominator == 0.0:
        raise ValueError("coordinates lie outside the quotient chart")
    a = r1 / r0
    lam = (r2 - r0**2) / denominator
    sigma1 = 1.0 - a + lam
    sigma2 = r0 * (1.0 - lam) - a + lam
    da = np.array([-r1 / r0**2, 1.0 / r0, 0.0])
    dlam = np.array(
        [2.0 * r0 * (lam - 1.0) / denominator, -lam / denominator, 1.0 / denominator]
    )
    dphi1 = -da + dlam
    dphi2 = np.array([1.0 - lam, 0.0, 0.0]) - da + (1.0 - r0) * dlam
    dphi = np.vstack([dphi1, dphi2])
    gradient = _analytic_log_divided_difference_gradient(sigma1, sigma2)
    de = da + gradient @ dphi
    ddiscriminant = np.array([2.0 * sigma1, -4.0]) @ dphi
    return np.vstack([de, ddiscriminant])


def _gap_masses(x: float, y: float, tolerance: float = 1e-15) -> FloatArray:
    masses: list[float] = []
    p = math.exp(-x)
    s = math.exp(-y)

    def survival(k: int) -> float:
        if math.isclose(x, y, rel_tol=1e-10, abs_tol=1e-14):
            theta = 0.5 * (x + y)
            return math.exp(-theta * k) * (1.0 + theta * k)
        return (y * p**k - x * s**k) / (y - x)

    previous = survival(0)
    for k in range(1_000_000):
        following = survival(k + 1)
        masses.append(previous - following)
        if following < tolerance:
            return np.asarray(masses)
        previous = following
    raise ArithmeticError("gap-law truncation did not converge")


def survival_factor_gap_masses(p: float, s: float, maximum_gap: int) -> FloatArray:
    """Return gap masses 0,...,maximum_gap-1 from survival factors p and s."""
    if not (0.0 < p < 1.0 and 0.0 < s < 1.0):
        raise ValueError("p and s must lie in (0,1)")
    if maximum_gap < 1:
        raise ValueError("maximum_gap must be positive")
    x = -math.log(p)
    y = -math.log(s)
    indices = np.arange(maximum_gap + 1, dtype=float)
    if math.isclose(x, y, rel_tol=1e-10, abs_tol=1e-14):
        rate = 0.5 * (x + y)
        survival = np.exp(-rate * indices) * (1.0 + rate * indices)
    else:
        survival = (y * p**indices - x * s**indices) / (y - x)
    return survival[:-1] - survival[1:]


def singular_gap_expansion(z: float, maximum_gap: int) -> tuple[FloatArray, FloatArray]:
    """Return g_z(k) and the d^2 coefficient for (p,s)=(z+d,z-d)."""
    if not 0.0 < z < 1.0:
        raise ValueError("z must lie in (0,1)")
    if maximum_gap < 1:
        raise ValueError("maximum_gap must be positive")
    k = np.arange(maximum_gap, dtype=float)
    log_z = math.log(z)
    denominator = 1.0 - z + log_z * (k * z - k + z)
    gap = z**k * denominator
    numerator = (
        k**3 * z * log_z
        - k**3 * log_z
        - 3.0 * k**2 * z
        + 3.0 * k**2 * log_z
        + 3.0 * k**2
        - 3.0 * k * z * log_z
        - 6.0 * k * z
        - 2.0 * z * log_z
        - 3.0 * z
    )
    coefficient = z**k * numerator / (6.0 * z**2)
    return gap, coefficient


def singular_gap_score(
    z: float, tolerance: float = 1e-15
) -> tuple[FloatArray, FloatArray, float, float]:
    """Return the diagonal gap law, singular score, cycle mean, and information."""
    if not 0.0 < tolerance < 1.0:
        raise ValueError("tolerance must lie in (0,1)")
    rate = -math.log(z)
    maximum_gap = 1
    while equal_rate_gap_tail(rate, maximum_gap) >= tolerance:
        maximum_gap += 1
        if maximum_gap > 1_000_000:
            raise ArithmeticError("gap-law truncation did not converge")
    gap, coefficient = singular_gap_expansion(z, maximum_gap)
    score = coefficient / gap
    mean_cycle = (1.0 - z - z * math.log(z)) / (1.0 - z) ** 2
    information = float(np.dot(gap, score**2) / mean_cycle)
    return gap, score, mean_cycle, information


def singular_symmetric_shift(z: float, h: float, sample_size: int) -> FloatArray:
    """Return sqrt(N) times the symmetric-coordinate shift of the N^-1/4 split."""
    if not 0.0 < z < 1.0 or not math.isfinite(h):
        raise ValueError("z must lie in (0,1) and h must be finite")
    if sample_size < 1:
        raise ValueError("sample_size must be positive")
    root_n = math.sqrt(sample_size)
    displacement = h / math.sqrt(root_n)
    p = z + displacement
    s = z - displacement
    if not (0.0 < p < 1.0 and 0.0 < s < 1.0):
        raise ValueError("the split survival factors must lie in (0,1)")
    return root_n * np.array([p + s - 2.0 * z, p * s - z**2])


def regenerative_inclusion_covariance(x: float, y: float) -> tuple[FloatArray, FloatArray]:
    """Deterministically sum the complete-cycle covariance from the gap law."""
    if min(x, y) <= 0.0:
        raise ValueError("dimensionless rates must be positive")
    g = _gap_masses(x, y)
    index = np.arange(g.size)
    mu = float(np.dot(index + 1.0, g))
    r = np.array([1.0 / mu, g[0] / mu, (g[1] + g[0] ** 2) / mu])
    i = index[:, None]
    j = index[None, :]
    reward = np.empty((g.size, g.size, 3))
    reward[:, :, 0] = 1.0
    reward[:, :, 1] = i == 0
    reward[:, :, 2] = (i == 1) + ((i == 0) & (j == 0))
    centered = reward - r[None, None, :] * (i[:, :, None] + 1.0)
    pair_mass = g[:, None] * g[None, :]
    variance = np.einsum("ij,ija,ijb->ab", pair_mass, centered, centered)
    future_mean = np.einsum("k,jka->ja", g, centered)
    adjacent = np.einsum("ij,ija,jb->ab", pair_mass, centered, future_mean)
    return r, (variance + adjacent + adjacent.T) / mu


def cone_wald_distance(e: float, discriminant: float, omega: ArrayLike) -> float:
    """Return the covariance-metric squared distance to {0} x [0,infinity)."""
    covariance = np.asarray(omega, dtype=float)
    if covariance.shape != (2, 2) or np.linalg.eigvalsh(covariance)[0] <= 0.0:
        raise ValueError("omega must be a positive-definite 2 by 2 matrix")
    beta = covariance[0, 1] / covariance[0, 0]
    variance = covariance[1, 1] - covariance[0, 1] ** 2 / covariance[0, 0]
    standardized_inequality = discriminant - beta * e
    return e**2 / covariance[0, 0] + min(standardized_inequality, 0.0) ** 2 / variance


def _chi_square_cdf(value: float, degrees: int) -> float:
    if value <= 0.0:
        return 0.0
    if degrees == 1:
        return math.erf(math.sqrt(value / 2.0))
    if degrees == 2:
        return 1.0 - math.exp(-value / 2.0)
    raise ValueError("only one and two degrees of freedom are used")


def boundary_critical_value(alpha: float) -> float:
    """Return the upper-alpha quantile of 0.5 chi1^2 + 0.5 chi2^2."""
    if not 0.0 < alpha < 1.0:
        raise ValueError("alpha must lie in (0,1)")
    lower, upper = 0.0, 1.0
    target = 1.0 - alpha
    while 0.5 * (_chi_square_cdf(upper, 1) + _chi_square_cdf(upper, 2)) < target:
        upper *= 2.0
    for _ in range(80):
        midpoint = 0.5 * (lower + upper)
        cdf = 0.5 * (_chi_square_cdf(midpoint, 1) + _chi_square_cdf(midpoint, 2))
        if cdf < target:
            lower = midpoint
        else:
            upper = midpoint
    return 0.5 * (lower + upper)


def local_gap_perturbation_basis() -> FloatArray:
    """Rows give the eta_mu, eta_0, eta_1 perturbations on gaps 0,...,3."""
    return np.array(
        [[0.0, 0.0, -1.0, 1.0], [1.0, 0.0, -3.0, 2.0], [0.0, 1.0, -2.0, 1.0]]
    )


def local_power(
    delta: float, tau: float, omega: ArrayLike, alpha: float = 0.05
) -> float:
    """Evaluate the joint image test's two-coordinate local-power integral."""
    covariance = np.asarray(omega, dtype=float)
    beta = covariance[0, 1] / covariance[0, 0]
    variance = covariance[1, 1] - covariance[0, 1] ** 2 / covariance[0, 0]
    a_star = delta / math.sqrt(covariance[0, 0])
    b_star = (tau - beta * delta) / math.sqrt(variance)
    critical = boundary_critical_value(alpha)

    with mp.workdps(40):
        root_critical = mp.sqrt(critical)

        def normal_cdf(value: mp.mpf) -> mp.mpf:
            return (1 + mp.erf(value / mp.sqrt(2))) / 2

        def interval_probability(q: mp.mpf) -> mp.mpf:
            if q <= 0:
                return mp.mpf("0")
            root = mp.sqrt(q)
            return normal_cdf(root - a_star) - normal_cdf(-root - a_star)

        positive_part = normal_cdf(b_star) * interval_probability(critical)
        negative_part = mp.quad(
            lambda value: interval_probability(critical - value**2)
            * mp.exp(-((value - b_star) ** 2) / 2)
            / mp.sqrt(2 * mp.pi),
            [-root_critical, 0],
        )
        return float(1 - positive_part - negative_part)

def symmetric_log_divided_difference(p: float, s: float) -> float:
    """Return the symmetric logarithmic divided difference C(p,s)."""
    if not (0.0 < p < 1.0 and 0.0 < s < 1.0):
        raise ValueError("p and s must lie in (0,1)")
    if math.isclose(p, s, rel_tol=1e-10, abs_tol=1e-14):
        m = 0.5 * (p + s)
        d = 0.5 * (p - s)
        return (
            m * (1.0 - math.log(m))
            + d**2 / m * (0.5 + math.log(m) / 3.0)
            + d**4 / m**3 * (1.0 / 12.0 + 4.0 * math.log(m) / 45.0)
        )
    return (s * math.log(p) - p * math.log(s)) / (math.log(p) - math.log(s))


def hidden_mode(x: float, y: float) -> float:
    """Return the continuous secant extension of the hidden eigenvalue."""
    if not (math.isfinite(x) and math.isfinite(y) and x > 0.0 and y > 0.0):
        raise ValueError("x and y must be finite and positive")
    if math.isclose(x, y, rel_tol=1e-10, abs_tol=1e-14):
        midpoint = 0.5 * (x + y)
        return math.exp(-midpoint) * (1.0 - midpoint)
    return (y * math.exp(-y) - x * math.exp(-x)) / (y - x)


def markov_gap_alternative(
    diagonal_rate: float, eta: float
) -> tuple[FloatArray, FloatArray, FloatArray, float]:
    """Construct the finite-range Markov-gap alternative at an exchange point."""
    if not math.isfinite(diagonal_rate) or diagonal_rate <= 0.0:
        raise ValueError("the diagonal rate must be finite and positive")
    if not math.isfinite(eta) or eta < 0.0:
        raise ValueError("eta must be finite and nonnegative")

    gap_mass = _gap_masses(diagonal_rate, diagonal_rate)
    gap_mass /= gap_mass.sum()
    if gap_mass.size < 3 or min(gap_mass[1], gap_mass[2]) <= 0.0:
        raise ArithmeticError("the truncated gap law does not contain positive masses 1 and 2")

    score = np.zeros_like(gap_mass)
    score[1] = 1.0
    score[2] = -gap_mass[1] / gap_mass[2]
    maximum_eta = gap_mass[2] / gap_mass[1]
    if eta >= maximum_eta:
        raise ValueError(f"eta must be smaller than {maximum_eta:.12g} for positivity")

    transition = gap_mass[None, :] * (
        1.0 + eta * score[:, None] * score[None, :]
    )
    indices = np.arange(gap_mass.size, dtype=float)
    mean_cycle = float((indices + 1.0) @ gap_mass)
    return gap_mass, score, transition, mean_cycle


def markov_gap_inclusions(
    gap_mass: ArrayLike, transition: ArrayLike, mean_cycle: float
) -> FloatArray:
    """Return the first three inclusion coordinates of a stationary gap chain."""
    g = np.asarray(gap_mass, dtype=float)
    q = np.asarray(transition, dtype=float)
    if g.ndim != 1 or g.size < 2 or q.shape != (g.size, g.size):
        raise ValueError("gap_mass and transition have incompatible shapes")
    if not math.isfinite(mean_cycle) or mean_cycle <= 0.0:
        raise ValueError("mean_cycle must be finite and positive")
    return np.array(
        [
            1.0 / mean_cycle,
            g[0] / mean_cycle,
            (g[1] + g[0] * q[0, 0]) / mean_cycle,
        ]
    )


def markov_gap_information(
    gap_mass: ArrayLike, score: ArrayLike, mean_cycle: float
) -> float:
    """Return Fisher information per calendar bin for the Markov-gap path."""
    g = np.asarray(gap_mass, dtype=float)
    h = np.asarray(score, dtype=float)
    if g.shape != h.shape or g.ndim != 1:
        raise ValueError("gap_mass and score must be one-dimensional arrays of equal size")
    if not math.isfinite(mean_cycle) or mean_cycle <= 0.0:
        raise ValueError("mean_cycle must be finite and positive")
    second_moment = float(g @ (h * h))
    return second_moment**2 / mean_cycle


def markov_gap_local_power(
    diagonal_rate: float, local_parameter: float, alpha: float = 0.05
) -> float:
    """Return the one-sided Gaussian local power of the finite-range score."""
    if not math.isfinite(local_parameter) or local_parameter < 0.0:
        raise ValueError("the local parameter must be finite and nonnegative")
    if not 0.0 < alpha < 1.0:
        raise ValueError("alpha must lie in (0,1)")
    g, h, _, mean_cycle = markov_gap_alternative(diagonal_rate, 0.0)
    information = markov_gap_information(g, h, mean_cycle)
    normal = NormalDist()
    critical = normal.inv_cdf(1.0 - alpha)
    return 1.0 - normal.cdf(critical - local_parameter * math.sqrt(information))


def _probability_vector(gap_mass: ArrayLike) -> FloatArray:
    g = np.asarray(gap_mass, dtype=float)
    if g.ndim != 1 or g.size < 2 or not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
        raise ValueError("gap_mass must be a finite one-dimensional vector")
    if np.min(g) <= 0.0 or not math.isclose(float(g.sum()), 1.0, abs_tol=1e-12):
        raise ValueError("gap_mass must be a strictly positive probability vector")
    return g


def markov_palm_tangent_projection(
    gap_mass: ArrayLike, values: ArrayLike
) -> FloatArray:
    """Project a finite table onto the fixed-marginal, q(0,0)=0 tangent space."""
    g = _probability_vector(gap_mass)
    table = np.asarray(values, dtype=float)
    if table.shape != (g.size, g.size) or not np.all(np.isfinite(table)):
        raise ValueError("values must be a finite square table matching gap_mass")

    row_mean = table @ g
    column_mean = g @ table
    interaction = table - row_mean[:, None] - column_mean[None, :] + g @ row_mean
    zero_contrast = np.zeros_like(g)
    zero_contrast[0] = 1.0
    zero_contrast -= g[0]
    atom_interaction = np.outer(zero_contrast, zero_contrast)
    atom_norm_squared = float(
        np.einsum("i,j,ij->", g, g, atom_interaction * atom_interaction)
    )
    coefficient = float(
        np.einsum("i,j,ij->", g, g, interaction * atom_interaction)
        / atom_norm_squared
    )
    return interaction - coefficient * atom_interaction


def project_markov_tangent(gap_mass: ArrayLike, values: ArrayLike) -> FloatArray:
    """Compatibility alias for :func:`markov_palm_tangent_projection`."""
    return markov_palm_tangent_projection(gap_mass, values)


def markov_palm_transition(
    gap_mass: ArrayLike, score: ArrayLike, parameter: float
) -> FloatArray:
    """Construct Q(l|k)=g(l){1+parameter*q(k,l)} on a finite gap space."""
    g = _probability_vector(gap_mass)
    q = np.asarray(score, dtype=float)
    if q.shape != (g.size, g.size) or not np.all(np.isfinite(q)):
        raise ValueError("score must be a finite square table matching gap_mass")
    if not math.isfinite(parameter):
        raise ValueError("parameter must be finite")
    transition = g[None, :] * (1.0 + parameter * q)
    if np.min(transition) < -1e-14:
        raise ValueError("parameter makes the transition kernel negative")
    return transition


def markov_palm_information(gap_mass: ArrayLike, score: ArrayLike) -> float:
    """Return the fixed-marginal Markov score information per calendar bin."""
    g = _probability_vector(gap_mass)
    q = np.asarray(score, dtype=float)
    if q.shape != (g.size, g.size) or not np.all(np.isfinite(q)):
        raise ValueError("score must be a finite square table matching gap_mass")
    mean_cycle = float((np.arange(g.size, dtype=float) + 1.0) @ g)
    norm_squared = float(np.einsum("i,j,ij->", g, g, q * q))
    return norm_squared / mean_cycle


def _weighted_mean_zero_basis(gap_mass: ArrayLike) -> FloatArray:
    g = _probability_vector(gap_mass)
    candidates = []
    zero_contrast = np.zeros_like(g)
    zero_contrast[0] = 1.0
    candidates.append(zero_contrast - g[0])
    for index in range(1, g.size):
        contrast = np.zeros_like(g)
        contrast[index] = 1.0
        candidates.append(contrast - g[index])

    basis: list[FloatArray] = []
    for candidate in candidates:
        residual = candidate.copy()
        for vector in basis:
            residual -= float(g @ (residual * vector)) * vector
        norm = math.sqrt(float(g @ (residual * residual)))
        if norm > 2e-12:
            basis.append(residual / norm)
        if len(basis) == g.size - 1:
            break
    return np.asarray(basis)


def finite_markov_tangent_basis(gap_mass: ArrayLike) -> FloatArray:
    """Return an orthonormal basis of the finite Markov-Palm tangent space."""
    one_gap_basis = _weighted_mean_zero_basis(gap_mass)
    products = [
        np.outer(left, right)
        for left_index, left in enumerate(one_gap_basis)
        for right_index, right in enumerate(one_gap_basis)
        if (left_index, right_index) != (0, 0)
    ]
    return np.asarray(products)


def equal_rate_gap_tail(rate: float, layer: int) -> float:
    """Return S_layer=exp(-rate*layer)*(1+rate*layer)."""
    if not math.isfinite(rate) or rate <= 0.0 or layer < 0:
        raise ValueError("rate must be positive and layer must be nonnegative")
    return math.exp(-rate * layer) * (1.0 + rate * layer)


def weighted_helmert_partition(rate: float, layer: int) -> tuple[FloatArray, FloatArray]:
    """Evaluate e_0,...,e_layer on atoms 0,...,layer and the residual tail."""
    if layer < 0:
        raise ValueError("layer must be nonnegative")
    tails = np.array([equal_rate_gap_tail(rate, j) for j in range(layer + 2)])
    masses = tails[:-1] - tails[1:]
    partition_mass = np.concatenate((masses, tails[-1:]))
    basis = np.zeros((layer + 1, layer + 2))
    for index in range(layer + 1):
        basis[index, index] = math.sqrt(
            tails[index + 1] / (masses[index] * tails[index])
        )
        basis[index, index + 1 :] = -math.sqrt(
            masses[index] / (tails[index] * tails[index + 1])
        )
    return partition_mass, basis


def helmert_layer_diagnostics(rate: float, layer: int) -> tuple[float, float, float]:
    """Return Gram error, S_J-scaled tensor envelope, and scaled third moment."""
    partition_mass, basis = weighted_helmert_partition(rate, layer)
    gram = np.einsum("k,ak,bk->ab", partition_mass, basis, basis)
    tensor_envelope = 0.0
    for left in range(layer + 1):
        for right in range(layer + 1):
            if (left, right) != (0, 0):
                tensor_envelope = max(
                    tensor_envelope,
                    float(np.max(np.abs(np.outer(basis[left], basis[right])))),
                )

    christoffel = np.sum(basis * basis, axis=0)
    omitted = np.outer(basis[0] * basis[0], basis[0] * basis[0])
    vector_norm_squared = np.outer(christoffel, christoffel) - omitted
    vector_third_moment = float(
        np.einsum(
            "i,j,ij->",
            partition_mass,
            partition_mass,
            np.maximum(vector_norm_squared, 0.0) ** 1.5,
        )
    )
    tail = equal_rate_gap_tail(rate, layer)
    return (
        float(np.max(np.abs(gram - np.eye(layer + 1)))),
        tail * tensor_envelope,
        tail * vector_third_moment,
    )


def _helmert_interaction_values(rate: float, layer: int) -> tuple[FloatArray, FloatArray]:
    partition_mass, basis = weighted_helmert_partition(rate, layer)
    values = np.asarray(
        [
            np.outer(basis[:, left], basis[:, right]).reshape(-1)[1:]
            for left in range(partition_mass.size)
            for right in range(partition_mass.size)
        ]
    ).reshape(partition_mass.size, partition_mass.size, -1)
    return partition_mass, values


def helmert_overlap_diagnostics(rate: float, layer: int) -> tuple[float, float, float]:
    """Return covariance error, lag-one covariance error, and S_J-scaled third moment."""
    partition_mass, values = _helmert_interaction_values(rate, layer)
    dimension = (layer + 1) ** 2 - 1
    covariance = np.einsum(
        "i,j,ija,ijb->ab", partition_mass, partition_mass, values, values
    )
    lag_covariance = np.einsum(
        "i,j,k,ija,jkb->ab",
        partition_mass,
        partition_mass,
        partition_mass,
        values,
        values,
    )
    third_moment = float(
        np.einsum(
            "i,j,ij->",
            partition_mass,
            partition_mass,
            np.linalg.norm(values, axis=2) ** 3,
        )
    )
    return (
        float(np.max(np.abs(covariance - np.eye(dimension)))),
        float(np.max(np.abs(lag_covariance))),
